Q8. The basic education advocated by Gandhiji in
Wardha Congress is related with
(a) Compulsory elementary education
(b) Social relevance of education to society
(c) Demand of separate institutions for minorities
(d) Compulsory military training for every student
Answer: (b)
     • At the Wardha Conference in 1937, Mahatma

Q5. Who can be called the father of local self-                   Secretary.
government in India?                                          •   Hence, option (b) is correct.
(a) Lord Ripon
(b) Lord Minto
(c) Lord Curzon
(d) Lord Dufferin
The correct option is (a).
     • Lord Ripon, who served as the Viceroy of
         India from 1880 to 1884, is widely regarded as
         the "father of local self-government in India."
     • In 1882, he introduced a series of reforms
         aimed at developing local bodies like
         municipal committees and district boards.
     • His resolution advocated for these bodies to
         have a majority of elected non-official
         members and to be entrusted with real
         financial and administrative powers.              Q7. The Indian national movement became
     • Ripon's intention was not just to improve           organised for the first time in
         administration but to provide political           (a) 1885
         education and training in self-governance for     (b) 1857
         Indians, laying a crucial foundation for          (c) 1905-06
         democratic institutions in the country.           (d) 1916-17

Q10. Uplift of the backward classes was the main                   British.
programme of the                                               • He mobilized the tribal communities and local
(a) Prathana Samaj                                                 zamindars, continuing his fight for many years
(b) Satyashodhak Samaj                                             even after the 1857 revolt was suppressed
(c) Arya Samaj                                                     elsewhere, making him a legendary figure of
(d) Ramakrishna Mission                                            resistance in the region.
The correct option is (b).                                     • Hence, option (b) is correct.
     • The Satyashodhak Samaj (Truth-Seekers'
         Society) was founded by Jyotiba Phule in
         Maharashtra in 1873.
     • The primary and central programme of this
         society was the social upliftment of the
         backward classes, particularly the Shudras
         and Ati-Shudras (Dalits).
     • Phule launched a powerful critique of the
         Brahmanical caste system, which he saw as
         the root cause of oppression. The Samaj
         worked to liberate the lower castes from the
         hegemony of the Brahmins by promoting
         education, social equality, and rational
         thought, and by challenging religious rituals
         and priestly dominance.
     • Hence, option (b) is correct.

Q7. Consider the following fruits:
     1. Papaya
     2. Pineapple
     3. Guava
How many of the above were introduced in India by
the Portuguese in the sixteenth and seventeenth
centuries?
(a) Only one
(b) Only two
(c) All the three
(d) None
The correct option is (c).
     • The Portuguese, during the Columbian
          Exchange, introduced several New World
          crops to India.
     • Papaya, pineapple, and guava were all
          brought from Central and South America to
          India by the Portuguese in the 16th–17th
          centuries.
